### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Azerbaijan)

See also: [Azerbaijan and the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_and_the_European_Union)



President [İlham Aliyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0lham_Aliyev) receiving the Supreme Order of the Turkic World from Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) during the 8th summit of the [Organization of Turkic States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_Turkic_States) in [Istanbul](3391396.md), Turkey, 12 November 2021

The short-lived Azerbaijan Democratic Republic succeeded in establishing diplomatic relations with six countries, sending diplomatic representatives to Germany and Finland.[^cite_note-CSUS-183] The process of international recognition of Azerbaijan's independence from the collapsing Soviet Union lasted roughly one year. As of 2007 the most recent country to recognize Azerbaijan was Bahrain, on 6 November 1996.[^cite_note-184] Full diplomatic relations, including mutual exchanges of missions, were first established with Turkey, Pakistan, the United States, Iran[^cite_note-CSUS-183] and Israel.[^cite_note-israelmfa-185] Azerbaijan has placed a particular emphasis on its "special relationship" with Turkey.[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187]

Azerbaijan has diplomatic relations with 158 countries so far and holds membership in 38 international organizations.[^cite_note-FCO-27] It holds observer status in the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) and [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) and is a correspondent at the [International Telecommunication Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Telecommunication_Union).[^cite_note-FCO-27] On 9 May 2006 Azerbaijan was elected to membership in the newly established [Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Council) by the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md). The term of office began on 19 June 2006.[^cite_note-188] Azerbaijan was first elected as a non-permanent member of the [UN Security Council in 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_election,_2011) with the support of 155 countries.



President [Ilham Aliyev](22765442.md) and other heads of state hosted by Kazakh President [Nursultan Nazarbayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nursultan_Nazarbayev) at the [Caspian Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caspian_Summit) in [Aktau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aktau), Kazakhstan, 12 August 2018

Foreign policy priorities include, first of all, the restoration of its territorial integrity; elimination of the consequences of occupation of Nagorno-Karabakh and seven other regions of Azerbaijan surrounding Nagorno-Karabakh;[^cite_note-NSC_AZ-189][^cite_note-190] integration into European and Euro-Atlantic structure; contribution to international security; cooperation with international organizations; regional cooperation and bilateral relations; strengthening of defence capability; promotion of security by domestic policy means; strengthening of democracy; preservation of ethnic and religious tolerance; scientific, educational, and cultural policy and preservation of moral values; economic and social development; enhancing internal and border security; and migration, energy, and transportation security policy.[^cite_note-NSC_AZ-189]

Azerbaijan is an active member of international coalitions fighting international terrorism, and was one of the first countries to offer support after the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md).[^cite_note-191] The country is an active member of [NATO](21133.md)'s [Partnership for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace) program, contributing to peacekeeping efforts in Kosovo, Afghanistan and Iraq. Azerbaijan is also a [member](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_the_Council_of_Europe) of the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) since 2001 and maintains good relations with the European Union. The country may eventually apply for EU membership.[^cite_note-NSC_AZ-189]



President Ilham Aliyev, US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and Armenian Prime Minister [Nikol Pashinyan](17897961.md) signing a trilateral [joint declaration](79518584.md) in Washington, D.C., 8 August 2025

On 1 July 2021, the US Congress advanced legislation that will have an impact on the military aid that Washington has sent to Azerbaijan since 2012. This was because the packages to Armenia, instead, are significantly smaller.[^cite_note-192]

Azerbaijan has been harshly criticized for bribing foreign officials and diplomats to promote its causes abroad and legitimize its elections at home, a practice termed [caviar diplomacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caviar_diplomacy).[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] The [Azerbaijani laundromat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_laundromat) [money laundering](19390.md) operation involved the bribery of foreign politicians and journalists to serve the Azerbaijani government's public relations interests.[^cite_note-197]

### Military

Main article: [Azerbaijani Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_Armed_Forces)



[Azerbaijani Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_Navy) ships during the 2022 Teknofest Azerbaijan festival in [Baku](4566.md)

The National Army of the Azerbaijan Democratic Republic was created on 26 June 1918.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199] When Azerbaijan gained independence after the dissolution of the Soviet Union, the Armed Forces of the Republic of Azerbaijan were created according to the law on the armed forces of 9 October 1991.[^cite_note-200] The original date of the establishment of the short-lived National Army is celebrated as Army Day (26 June).[^cite_note-201] As of 2021, Azerbaijan had 126,000 active personnel in its armed forces. There are also 17,000 paramilitary troops and 330,00 reserve personnel.[^cite_note-Blandy12-202] The armed forces have three branches: the [Land Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_Azerbaijan), the [Air Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_Air_Forces) and the [Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_Navy). Additionally the armed forces embrace several military sub-groups that can be involved in state defence when needed. These are the [Internal Troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Troops_of_Azerbaijan) of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and the [State Border Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Border_Service_of_Azerbaijan), which includes the Coast Guard as well.[^cite_note-cia-30] The [Azerbaijani National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_National_Guard) is a paramilitary force that operates as a semi-independent entity of the Special State Protection Service, an agency subordinate to the president.[^cite_note-203]



Members of the [Special Forces of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Forces_of_Azerbaijan) during the [Baku Victory Parade of 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Victory_Parade_of_2020)

Azerbaijan adheres to the [Treaty on Conventional Armed Forces in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_on_Conventional_Armed_Forces_in_Europe) and has signed all major international arms and weapons treaties. Azerbaijan closely cooperates with [NATO](21133.md) in programs such as [Partnership for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace) and [Individual Partnership Action Plan/pfp and ipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual_Partnership_Action_Plan). Azerbaijan has deployed 151 of its peacekeeping forces in Iraq and another 184 in Afghanistan.[^cite_note-204]

Azerbaijan spent \$2.24 billion on its defence budget as of 2020,[^cite_note-205] which amounted to 5.4% of its total GDP,[^cite_note-206] and some 12.7% of general government expenditure.[^cite_note-207] Azerbaijani [defence industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_Industry_of_Azerbaijan) manufactures small arms, artillery systems, tanks, armours and [night vision devices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_vision_device), aviation bombs, UAVs/unmanned aerial vehicle, various military vehicles and military planes and helicopters.[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211]

### Human rights and freedom

Main article: [Human rights in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Azerbaijan)



Human rights activist [Rufat Safarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rufat_Safarov), who was arrested on December 4, 2024

The constitution claims to guarantee freedom of speech, but this is denied in practice. After several years of decline in press and media freedom, in 2014, the media environment deteriorated rapidly under a governmental campaign to silence any opposition and criticism, even while the country led the Committee of Ministers of the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) (May–November 2014). Spurious legal charges and impunity in violence against journalists have remained the norm.[^cite_note-pace-212] All foreign broadcasts are banned in the country.[^cite_note-FH-213] According to the 2013 [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) Freedom of the Press report, Azerbaijan's press freedom status is "not free", and Azerbaijan ranks 177th out of 196 countries.[^cite_note-214] In 2025, there were 375 political prisoners in Azerbaijan.[^cite_note-215] [Radio Free Europe/Radio Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Free_Europe/Radio_Liberty) and [Voice of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_America) are banned in Azerbaijan.[^cite_note-216] Discrimination against [LGBT people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Azerbaijan) in Azerbaijan is widespread.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218]

A report by an [Amnesty International](18947898.md) researcher in October 2015 points to "...the severe deterioration of human rights in Azerbaijan over the past few years. Sadly Azerbaijan has been allowed to get away with unprecedented levels of repression and in the process almost wipe out its civil society."[^cite_note-219] Amnesty's 2015/16 annual report[^cite_note-220] on the country stated "... persecution of political dissent continued. Human rights organizations remained unable to resume their work. At least 18 prisoners of conscience remained in detention at the end of the year. Reprisals against independent journalists and activists persisted both in the country and abroad, while their family members also faced harassment and arrests. International human rights monitors were barred and expelled from the country. Reports of torture and other ill-treatment persisted."[^cite_note-221]

### Corruption

Main article: [Corruption in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_Azerbaijan)

*[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported in April 2017 that "Azerbaijan's ruling elite operated a secret \$2.9bn (£2.2bn) scheme to pay prominent Europeans, buy luxury goods and launder money through a network of opaque British companies .... Leaked data shows that the Azerbaijani leadership, accused of serial human rights abuses, systemic corruption and rigging elections, made more than 16,000 covert payments from 2012 to 2014. Some of this money went to politicians and journalists, as part of an international lobbying operation to deflect criticism of Azerbaijan's president, Ilham Aliyev, and to promote a positive image of his oil-rich country." There was no suggestion that all recipients were aware of the source of the money as it arrived via a disguised route.[^cite_note-Gdn4917-222]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Azerbaijan)



Change in per capita GDP of Azerbaijan since 1973. Figures are inflation-adjusted to 2011 International dollars.

After gaining independence in 1991, Azerbaijan became a member of the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md), the [World Bank](36752.md), the [European Bank for Reconstruction and Development](37963.md), the [Islamic Development Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Development_Bank), and the [Asian Development Bank](2512.md).[^cite_note-AZE-223] The banking system consists of the [Central Bank of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Azerbaijan), [commercial banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_bank), and non-banking credit organizations. The National (now Central) Bank was created in 1992 based on the Azerbaijan State Savings Bank, an affiliate of the former State Savings Bank of the USSR. The Central Bank serves as Azerbaijan's central bank, empowered to issue the national currency, the [Azerbaijani manat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_manat), and to supervise all commercial banks. Two major commercial banks are [UniBank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unibank_\(Azerbaijan\)) and the state-owned [International Bank of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Bank_of_Azerbaijan), run by Abbas Ibrahimov.[^cite_note-224]

Pushed up by spending and demand growth, the 2007 [Q1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_quarter_of_a_calendar_year) inflation rate reached 16.6%.[^cite_note-INF-225] Nominal incomes and monthly wages climbed 29% and 25% respectively against this figure, but price increases in the non-oil industry encouraged inflation.[^cite_note-INF-225] Azerbaijan shows some signs of the so-called "[Dutch disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_disease)" because of its fast-growing energy sector, which causes inflation and makes non-energy exports more expensive.[^cite_note-226] In the early 2000s, chronically high inflation was brought under control. This led to the launch of a new currency, the new Azerbaijani manat, on 1 January 2006, to cement the economic reforms and erase the vestiges of an unstable economy.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228] By 2012 the GDP of Azerbaijan had increased 20-fold from its 1995 level.[^cite_note-229]

### Energy and natural resources

Main article: [Energy in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Azerbaijan)

Further information: [Petroleum industry in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroleum_industry_in_Azerbaijan)



[Oil Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_Rocks) (Neft Daşları) near Baku

Two-thirds of Azerbaijan is rich in oil and natural gas.[^cite_note-AZ-230] The oil industry dates back to the ancient period. Arabian historian and traveller [Ahmad Al-Baladhuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Baladhuri) discusses the economy of the Absheron Peninsula in antiquity, mentioning its oil in particular.[^cite_note-231] There are many [pipelines in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pipelines_in_Azerbaijan). The goal of the [Southern Gas Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Gas_Corridor), which connects the giant [Shah Deniz gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Deniz_gas_field) in Azerbaijan to Europe,[^cite_note-naturalgas-232] is to reduce European Union's dependency on Russian gas.[^cite_note-233]

The region of the Lesser Caucasus accounts for most of the country's gold, silver, iron, copper, [titanium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanium), [chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium), [manganese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manganese), [cobalt](24580536.md), [molybdenum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molybdenum), complex [ore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ore) and [antimony](898.md).[^cite_note-AZ-230] In September 1994, a 30-year contract was signed between the State Oil Company of Azerbaijan Republic ([SOCAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOCAR)) and 13 oil companies, among them [Amoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amoco), [BP](18998720.md), [ExxonMobil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ExxonMobil), [Lukoil](1027881.md) and [Equinor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equinor).[^cite_note-AZE-223] Western oil companies have been able to tap deepwater [oilfields](2910801.md) untouched by the Soviet exploitation. International academics consider Azerbaijan as one of the most important [oil exploration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrocarbon_exploration) and development regions.[^cite_note-234] The [State Oil Fund of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Oil_Fund_of_Azerbaijan) was established as an extra-budgetary fund to ensure [macroeconomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macroeconomy) stability, transparency in the management of oil revenue, and safeguarding of resources for future generations.



The [South Caucasus Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Caucasus_Pipeline) bringing natural gas through Turkey to Europe

Access to [biocapacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biocapacity) is less than world average. In 2016, Azerbaijan had 0.8 global hectares[^cite_note-footprintdata-235] of biocapacity per person within its territory, half the world average of 1.6 global hectares per person.[^cite_note-236] In 2016 Azerbaijan used 2.1 global hectares of biocapacity per person – their [ecological footprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecological_footprint) of consumption. This means they use more biocapacity than Azerbaijan contains. As a result, Azerbaijan is running a biocapacity deficit.[^cite_note-footprintdata-235]

Azeriqaz, a sub-company of SOCAR, intends to ensure full gasification of the country by 2021.[^cite_note-237] Azerbaijan was one of the sponsors of the east–west and north–south energy transport corridors. [Baku–Tbilisi–Kars railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku%E2%80%93Tbilisi%E2%80%93Kars_railway) line connects the Caspian region with Turkey. The [Trans-Anatolian gas pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Anatolian_gas_pipeline) and [Trans Adriatic Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans_Adriatic_Pipeline) deliver natural gas from Azerbaijan's Shah Deniz gas to Turkey and Europe.[^cite_note-naturalgas-232] Azerbaijan extended the agreement on development of [ACG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azeri%E2%80%93Chirag%E2%80%93Gunashli) until 2050 according to the amended [PSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Production_sharing_agreement) signed on 14 September 2017 by SOCAR and co-ventures ([BP](18998720.md), [Chevron](284749.md), [Inpex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inpex), [Equinor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equinor), [ExxonMobil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ExxonMobil), [TP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%BCrkiye_Petrolleri_Anonim_Ortakl%C4%B1%C4%9F%C4%B1), ITOCHU and [ONGC Videsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_and_Natural_Gas_Corporation)).[^cite_note-238]

### Agriculture

Main article: [Agriculture in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Azerbaijan)

Azerbaijan has the largest agricultural basin in the region. About 54.9 percent of Azerbaijan is agricultural land.[^cite_note-STA-137] At the beginning of 2007 there were 4,755,100 hectares of used agricultural area.[^cite_note-AG-239] In the same year the total wood resources counted 136 million m<sup>3</sup>.[^cite_note-AG-239] Agricultural scientific research institutes are focused on meadows and pastures, horticulture and subtropical crops, green vegetables, [viticulture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viticulture) and [wine-making](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_wine), cotton growing and [medicinal plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicinal_plants).[^cite_note-240] In some areas it is profitable to grow grain, potatoes, [sugar beets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_beet), cotton[^cite_note-241] and tobacco. Livestock, dairy products, and wine and [spirits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distilled_spirit) are also important farm products. The Caspian [fishing industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing_industry) concentrates on the dwindling stocks of [sturgeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sturgeon) and [beluga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beluga_\(sturgeon\)). In 2002 the Azerbaijani [merchant marine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merchant_marine) had 54 ships.

Some products previously imported from abroad have begun to be produced locally. Among them are Coca-Cola by Coca-Cola Bottlers LTD., beer by Baki-Kastel, parquet by Nehir and oil pipes by EUPEC Pipe Coating Azerbaijan.[^cite_note-242]

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Azerbaijan)



[Shahdag Mountain Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahdag_Mountain_Resort) is the country's largest winter resort.

The country was a well-known tourist spot in the 1980s. The fall of the Soviet Union and the First Nagorno-Karabakh War during the 1990s damaged the tourist industry and the image of Azerbaijan as a tourist destination.[^cite_note-243] It was not until the 2000s that the tourism industry began to recover, and the country has since experienced a high rate of growth in the number of tourist visits and overnight stays.[^cite_note-244] In recent years, Azerbaijan has also become a popular destination for religious, spa, and [health care tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_medical_tourism).[^cite_note-Baku_Boom_Has_Yet_to_Hit_Regions-245] During winter, the [Shahdag Mountain Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahdag_Mountain_Resort) offers skiing with state of the art facilities.[^cite_note-246]

The government has set development as an elite tourist destination as a top priority. It is a national strategy to make tourism a major, if not the single largest, contributor to the Azerbaijani economy.[^cite_note-247] These activities are regulated by the [Ministry of Culture and Tourism of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_and_Tourism_\(Azerbaijan\)). There are 63 countries which have a visa-free score.[^cite_note-248] E-visa[^cite_note-249] – for a visit of foreigners of visa-required countries to the Republic of Azerbaijan. According to the Travel and Tourism Competitiveness Report 2015 of the [World Economic Forum](172185.md), Azerbaijan holds 84th place.[^cite_note-250]

According to a report by the World Travel and Tourism Council, Azerbaijan was among the top ten countries showing the strongest growth in visitor exports between 2010 and 2016.[^cite_note-251] In addition, Azerbaijan placed first (46.1%) among countries with the fastest-developing travel and tourism economies, with strong indicators for inbound international visitor spending in 2016.[^cite_note-252]



Panoramic view of [Baku](4566.md), the capital and largest city of Azerbaijan

### Transportation

Main articles: [Transportation in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Azerbaijan) and [Rail transport in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Azerbaijan)

The convenient location of Azerbaijan on the crossroad of major international traffic arteries, such as the [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road) and the south–north corridor, highlights the strategic importance of the transportation sector for the country's economy.[^cite_note-CACISRSSilkRoads-253] The transport sector includes roads, railways, aviation, and maritime transport. It is also an important economic hub in the transportation of raw materials. The [Baku–Tbilisi–Ceyhan pipeline](397567.md) (BTC) became operational in May 2006 and extends more than 1,774 km (1,102 mi) through the territories of Azerbaijan, Georgia, and Turkey. The BTC is designed to transport up to 50 million tons of crude oil annually and carries oil from the Caspian Sea oilfields to global markets.[^cite_note-254] The [South Caucasus Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Caucasus_Pipeline), also stretching through the territory of Azerbaijan, Georgia, and Turkey, became operational at the end of 2006 and offers additional gas supplies to the European market from the [Shah Deniz gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Deniz_gas_field). Shah Deniz is expected to produce up to 296 billion cubic meters of natural gas per year.[^cite_note-255] Azerbaijan also plays a major role in the EU-sponsored Silk Road Project.[^cite_note-CACISRSSilkRoads-253]

In 2002, the government established the Ministry of Transport with a broad range of policy and regulatory functions. In the same year, the country became a member of the [Vienna Convention on Road Traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna_Convention_on_Road_Traffic).[^cite_note-256] Priorities are upgrading the transport network and improving transportation services to better facilitate the development of other sectors of the economy. The 2012 construction of Kars–Tbilisi–Baku railway was meant to improve transportation between Asia and Europe by connecting the railways of China and Kazakhstan in the east to the European railway system in the west via Turkey. In 2010 [Broad-gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_gauge) railways and electrified railways stretched for 2,918 km (1,813 mi) and 1,278 km (794 mi) respectively. By 2010, there were 35 airports and one [heliport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heliport).[^cite_note-cia-30]

### Science and technology

Main articles: [Science and technology in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Azerbaijan), [Communications in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications_in_Azerbaijan), [Azerbaijan National Aerospace Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_National_Aerospace_Agency), and [List of Azerbaijani inventions and discoveries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Azerbaijani_inventions_and_discoveries)



[Shamakhi Astrophysical Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamakhi_Astrophysical_Observatory)

In the 21st century, a new oil and gas boom helped improve the situation in the science and technology sectors. The government launched a campaign aimed at modernization and innovation. The government estimates that profits from the information technology and communication industry will grow and become comparable to those from oil production.[^cite_note-257] Azerbaijan has a large and steadily growing Internet sector. In 2012, rapid growth was forecast for at least five more years.[^cite_note-258] Azerbaijan was ranked 95th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2024.[^cite_note-259] and moved up to the 94th rank in 2025.[^cite_note-260]

The country has been making progress in developing its telecoms sector. The Ministry of Communications & Information Technologies and an operator through its role in Aztelekom are both policy-makers and regulators. Public payphones are available for local calls and require the purchase of a token from the telephone exchange or some shops and kiosks. Tokens allow a call of indefinite duration. As of 2009, there were 1,397,000 main telephone lines[^cite_note-261] and 1,485,000 internet users.[^cite_note-262] There are four [GSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSM) providers: [Azercell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azercell), [Bakcell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakcell), Azerfon ([Nar Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nar_Mobile)), [Nakhtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakhtel) mobile network operators and one [CDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CDMA).

In the 21st century several prominent Azerbaijani [geodynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geodynamics) and [geotectonics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geotectonics) scientists, inspired by the fundamental works of Elchin Khalilov and others, designed hundreds of earthquake prediction stations and earthquake-resistant buildings that now constitute the bulk of The Republican Center of Seismic Service.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] The [Azerbaijan National Aerospace Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_National_Aerospace_Agency) launched its first satellite [AzerSat 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AzerSat_1) into orbit on 7 February 2013 from [Guiana Space Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guiana_Space_Centre) in French Guiana at orbital positions 46° East.[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] The satellite covers Europe and a significant part of Asia and Africa and serves the transmission of TV and radio broadcasting as well as the Internet.[^cite_note-269] The launching of a satellite into orbit is Azerbaijan's first step in realizing its goal of becoming a nation with its own space industry, capable of successfully implementing more projects in the future.[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271]

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Azerbaijan) and [List of cities in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Azerbaijan)



Population pyramid

As of March 2022, 52.9% of the population of 10,164,464 is urban, with the remaining 47.1% being rural.[^cite_note-reportpop-272] In January 2019, 50.1% of the total population was female. The [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio) in the same year was 0.99 males per female.[^cite_note-273] The 2011 population growth-rate was 0.85%, compared to 1.09% worldwide.[^cite_note-cia-30] A significant factor restricting population growth is a high level of migration. In 2011 Azerbaijan saw a migration of −1.14/1,000 people.[^cite_note-cia-30]

The [Azerbaijani diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_diaspora) is found in 42 countries[^cite_note-274] and in turn there are many centres for ethnic minorities inside Azerbaijan, including the [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germans_in_Azerbaijan) cultural society "Karelhaus", [Slavic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavic_peoples) cultural centre, Azerbaijani-Israeli community, [Kurdish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_people) cultural centre, International [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_people) Association, [Lezgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgins) national centre "Samur", Azerbaijani-[Tatar](67135.md) community, and [Crimean Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_Tatars) society.[^cite_note-MIN-275]

In total, Azerbaijan has 78 cities, 63 city districts, and one special legal status city. 261 urban-type settlements and 4248 villages follow these.[^cite_note-stat.gov.az-276]

Main articles: [Azerbaijanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijanis) and [Ethnic minorities in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_minorities_in_Azerbaijan)

Ethnic groups in Azerbaijan (2019 census)

2. [Lezgins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgins) (1.70%)

3. [Talyshs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talyshs) (0.90%)

The ethnic composition of the population according to the 2019 population census: 94.8% [Azerbaijanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_people), 1.7% [Lezgins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgians), 0.9% [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_people), 0.7% [Russians](59510.md), 0.5% [Avars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avars_\(Caucasus\)), 0.4% [Turks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_people), 0.3% [Tats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tat_people_\(Caucasus\)), 0.2% [Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars), 0.1% [Ukrainians](145144.md), 0.1% [Tsakhurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsakhur_people), 0.1% [Georgians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgians), 0.1% [Jews](25955086.md), and 0.2% others.[^cite_note-auto-279]

### Languages

Main article: [Languages of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Azerbaijan)

The official language is [Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language), a [Turkic language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkic_languages). Approximately 96% of the national population speak it as their [mother tongue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_tongue). Russian is still spoken in Azerbaijan. It is the mother tongue of 0.8% of the national population.[^cite_note-280] In 1989, Armenian was the majority language in the region of Nagorno-Karabakh, spoken by about 76% of the regional population.[^cite_note-281] After the first Nagorno-Karabakh war, native speakers of Armenian composed around 95% of the regional population.[^cite_note-282]

A dozen other minority languages are spoken natively,[^cite_note-283] including [Avar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avar_language), [Budukh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budukh_language),[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] [Georgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_language), [Juhuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juhuri_language),[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] [Khinalug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khinalug_language),[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] [Kryts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryts_language),[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] [Lezgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgian_language), [Rutul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutul_language),[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_language), [Tat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tat_language_\(Caucasus\)),[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] [Tsakhur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsakhur_language),[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] and [Udi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udi_language).[^cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-284] All these are spoken only by small minority populations, some of which are tiny and decreasing.[^cite_note-285]

### Religion

Main articles: [Religion in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Azerbaijan) and [Irreligion in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_Azerbaijan)



The [Bibi-Heybat Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibi-Heybat_Mosque) in Baku. The mosque is built over the tomb of a descendant of [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad).

[^cite_note-Bibi-286]

Azerbaijan is considered the most secular Muslim-majority country.[^cite_note-287] Around 97% of the population are Muslims.[^cite_note-288] Around 55–65% of Muslims are estimated to be [Shia](26961.md), while 45–55% of Muslims are [Sunnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Muslim).[^cite_note-Pres-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291] Azerbaijani Sunnis mainly follow the [Hanafi school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanafi_school).[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] The secular government of Azerbaijan officially promoted Turkish-influenced Sufi version of Sunni Islam over both Iranian-influenced Shia Islam, but maintained vigilance over religious activities of any sect.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] A such, there is an ongoing rise in conversions to Sunni Islam, which is seen as a symbol of growing closeness with Turkey.[^cite_note-296] Other faiths are practised by the country's various ethnic groups. Under article 48 of its constitution, Azerbaijan is a [secular state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_state) and ensures [religious freedom](46006.md). In a 2006–2008 [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_\(company\)) poll, only 21% of respondents from Azerbaijan stated that religion is an important part of their daily lives.[^cite_note-297]

Of the nation's religious minorities, the estimated 280,000 Christians (3.1%)[^cite_note-298] are mostly [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church) and [Georgian](433340.md) [Orthodox](10186.md) and [Armenian Apostolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church) (almost all Armenians live in the break-away region of [Nagorno-Karabakh](65220.md)).[^cite_note-cia-30] In 2003, there were 250 [Roman Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholics).[^cite_note-299] Other Christian denominations as of 2002 include [Lutherans](23371382.md), [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists) and [Molokans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molokan).[^cite_note-AZR-300] There is also a small [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) community.[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] Azerbaijan also has an ancient Jewish population with a [2,000-year history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Jews); Jewish organizations estimate that 12,000 Jews remain in Azerbaijan, which is home to the [only Jewish-majority town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Q%C4%B1rm%C4%B1z%C4%B1_Q%C9%99s%C9%99b%C9%99) outside of Israel and the United States.[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] Azerbaijan also is home to members of the [Baháʼí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith), [Hare Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Society_for_Krishna_Consciousness) and [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah's_Witnesses) communities, as well as adherents of the other religious communities.[^cite_note-AZR-300] All religious communities are required to register to be allowed to meet, under the risk of imprisonment. This registration is often denied.[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] Some religious communities have been [unofficially restricted from religious freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_Azerbaijan#Restrictions_on_religious_freedom). A [U.S. State Department](31975.md) report on the matter mentions detention of members of certain Muslim and Christian groups, and many groups have difficulty registering with the agency who regulates religion, [The State Committee on Religious Associations of the Republic of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Committee_on_Affairs_with_Religious_Associations_of_the_Republic_of_Azerbaijan).[^cite_note-309]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Azerbaijan)



Classroom in [Dunya School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khazar_University)

A relatively high percentage of Azerbaijanis have obtained some form of higher education, most notably in scientific and technical subjects.[^cite_note-AZ_study-310] In the Soviet era, literacy and average education levels rose dramatically from their very low starting point, despite two changes in the standard alphabet, from [Perso-Arabic script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perso-Arabic_script) to [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_script) in the 1920s and from Roman to [Cyrillic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrillic) in the 1930s. According to Soviet data, 100 percent of males and females (ages nine to forty-nine) were literate in 1970.[^cite_note-AZ_study-310] According to the [United Nations Development Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Program) Report 2009, the literacy rate is 99.5 percent.[^cite_note-311]

Since independence, one of the first laws that Parliament passed to disassociate itself from the Soviet Union was to adopt a [modified-Latin alphabet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_alphabet) to replace Cyrillic.[^cite_note-312] Other than that the Azerbaijani system has undergone little structural change. Initial alterations have included the reestablishment of religious education (banned during the Soviet period) and curriculum changes that have reemphasized the use of the Azerbaijani language and have eliminated ideological content. In addition to elementary schools, the education institutions include thousands of preschools, general secondary schools, and [vocational schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_schools), including specialized secondary schools and technical schools. Education through the ninth grade is compulsory.[^cite_note-\:02-313]

## Culture

The [Azerbaijani carpet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_rug) and [Kalaghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalaghai), a [UNESCO](21786641.md) [Masterpiece of Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity)

An Azerbaijani carpet from the Shirvan group from Bijo village, mid-19th century

Silk Kalaghais

The [culture of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Azerbaijan) has developed as a result of many influences; that is why Azerbaijanis are, in many ways, [bi-cultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biculturalism). National traditions are preserved despite Western influences, including globalized consumer culture. For example, [Novruz Bayram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novruz_in_Azerbaijan) is a family holiday derived from the traditional celebration of the New Year in [Zoroastrianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism).[^cite_note-314]

Azerbaijani national and traditional dresses are the [chokha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chokha) and [papakhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papakhi). There are radio broadcasts in Russian, [Georgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_language), [Kurdish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_language), [Lezgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgian_language) and [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_language) languages, which are financed from the state budget.[^cite_note-MIN-275] Some local radio stations in [Balakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balak%C9%99n) and [Khachmaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khachmaz_\(city\)) organize broadcasts in [Avar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avar_language) and [Tat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tat_language_\(Caucasus\)).[^cite_note-MIN-275] In Baku several newspapers are published in Russian, Kurdish (*Dengi Kurd*), Lezgian (*Samur*) and Talysh languages.[^cite_note-MIN-275] A Jewish society called "Sokhnut" publishes the newspaper *Aziz*.[^cite_note-MIN-275]

### Architecture



[Baku White City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_White_City) in [Baku](4566.md) was opened in 2011 and completed in 2022.

[Azerbaijani architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Azerbaijan) typically combines elements of [East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_world) and [West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_culture),[^cite_note-315] with heavy influences from [Persian architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_architecture). Many ancient architectural treasures are preserved, such as the [Maiden Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maiden_Tower_\(Baku\)) and [Palace of the Shirvanshahs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Shirvanshahs) in the Walled City of Baku. Entries on the [UNESCO World Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage) tentative list include the [Ateshgah of Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ateshgah_of_Baku), [Momine Khatun Mausoleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momine_Khatun_Mausoleum), [Hirkan National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirkan_National_Park), [Binagadi asphalt lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binagadi_asphalt_lake), [Lökbatan Mud Volcano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%B6kbatan_Mud_Volcano), [Shusha State Historical and Architectural Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shusha_State_Historical_and_Architectural_Reserve), Baku Stage Mountain, Caspian Shore Defensive Constructions, Ordubad National Reserve and the [Palace of Shaki Khans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Shaki_Khans).[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317]

Among other architectural treasures are [Quadrangular Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrangular_castle_\(Mardakan\)) in [Mardakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mardakan), [Parigala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parigala) in [Yukhary Chardaglar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuxar%C4%B1_%C3%87ardaqlar), several bridges spanning the Aras River, and several mausoleums. In the 19th and early 20th centuries, little monumental architecture was created, but distinctive residences were built in Baku and elsewhere. Among the most recent architectural monuments, the [Baku subways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Metro) are noted for their lavish decor.[^cite_note-318]

The task for modern Azerbaijani architecture is diverse application of modern aesthetics, the search for an architect's own artistic style and inclusion of the existing historico-cultural environment. Major projects such as [Heydar Aliyev Cultural Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heydar_Aliyev_Cultural_Center), [Flame Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flame_Towers), [Baku Crystal Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Crystal_Hall), [Baku White City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_White_City) and [SOCAR Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOCAR_Tower) have transformed the country's skyline and promotes its contemporary identity.[^cite_note-319]

### Music and dance



[Uzeyir Hajibeyov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzeyir_Hajibeyov) merged traditional [Azerbaijani music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_music) with Western styles in the early 20th century.

[Music of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Azerbaijan) builds on [folk traditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music) that reach back nearly a thousand years,[^cite_note-320] evolving around the badge of [monody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monody), producing rhythmically diverse melodies.[^cite_note-EMD-321] The music has a branchy [mode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_mode) system, where [chromatization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromaticism) of [major and minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_and_minor) [scales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_scale) is of great importance.[^cite_note-EMD-321] Among national musical instruments there are 14 [string instruments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_instrument), eight percussion instruments and six wind instruments.[^cite_note-322] According to *[The Grove Dictionary of Music and Musicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Grove_Dictionary_of_Music_and_Musicians)*, "in terms of ethnicity, culture and religion the Azerbaijani are musically much closer to Iran than Turkey."[^cite_note-323]



[Alim Qasimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alim_Qasimov) performs [mugham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugham) at [Eurovision Song Contest 2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2012). The Azerbaijani Mugham was inscribed in 2008 as a [UNESCO](21786641.md) [Masterpiece of Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity).

[Mugham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugham) is usually a suite with poetry and instrumental interludes. When performing mugham, the singers have to transform their emotions into singing and music. In contrast to the mugham traditions of Central Asian countries, Azerbaijani mugham is more free-form and less rigid; it is often compared to the improvised field of [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz).[^cite_note-324] [UNESCO](21786641.md) proclaimed the Azerbaijani mugham tradition a [Masterpiece of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity). [Meykhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meykhana) is a kind of traditional Azerbaijani distinctive folk unaccompanied song, usually performed by several people improvising on a particular subject.[^cite_note-325]

[Ashiq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashik) combines poetry, storytelling, dance, and vocal and instrumental music into a traditional performance art that stands as a symbol of Azerbaijani culture. It is a mystic [troubadour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troubadour) or travelling bard who sings and plays the [saz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baglama). This tradition has its origin in the [shamanistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamanism) beliefs of ancient [Turkic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkic_peoples).[^cite_note-European_University_Institute,_Florence,_Italy-326] Ashiqs' songs are semi-improvised around common bases. Azerbaijan's ashiq art was included in the list of [Intangible Cultural Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intangible_Cultural_Heritage) by UNESCO in 2009.[^cite_note-327]

Since the mid-1960s, Western-influenced [Azerbaijani pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_pop_music), in its various forms, that has been growing in popularity in Azerbaijan, while genres such as [rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_rock) and [hip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_hip_hop) are widely produced and enjoyed. Azerbaijani pop and [Azerbaijani folk music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_folk_music) arose with the international popularity of performers like [Alim Qasimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alim_Qasimov), [Rashid Behbudov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashid_Behbudov), [Vagif Mustafazadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vagif_Mustafazadeh), [Muslim Magomayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Magomayev_\(musician\)), [Shovkat Alakbarova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shovkat_Alakbarova) and [Rubaba Muradova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubaba_Muradova).[^cite_note-328] Azerbaijan is an enthusiastic participant in the Eurovision Song Contest. Azerbaijan made its debut appearance at the 2008 [Eurovision Song Contest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2008). The [country's entry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest_2009) gained third place in 2009 and fifth the following year.[^cite_note-329] [Ell and Nikki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eldar_&_Nigar) won the first place at the [Eurovision Song Contest 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2011) with the song "[Running Scared](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running_Scared_\(Ell_&_Nikki_song\))", entitling Azerbaijan to host the contest in [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2012), in Baku.[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] They have qualified for every Grand Final up until the [2018 edition of the contest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2018), entering with [X My Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_My_Heart_\(song\)) by singer [Aisel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aisel_\(singer\)).[^cite_note-332]

There are dozens of [Azerbaijani folk dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_dances). They are performed at formal celebrations and the dancers wear national clothes like the chokha, which is well-preserved within the national dances. Most dances have a very fast rhythm.[^cite_note-333]

### Art



Traditional Azerbaijani clothing and musical instruments

[Azerbaijani art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_art) is represented by a wide range of handicrafts, such as [chasing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Repouss%C3%A9_and_chasing), jewelling, engraving in metal, carving in wood, stone, or bone, carpet-making, lasing, pattern weaving and printing, and knitting and embroidery. Each of these types of decorative art, evidence of the endowments of the Azerbaijan nation, is very much in favour here. Many interesting facts pertaining to the development of arts and crafts in Azerbaijan were reported by numerous merchants, travellers, and diplomats who had visited these places at different times.[^cite_note-334]

The [Azerbaijani carpet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_rug) is a traditional handmade textile of various sizes, with a dense texture and a pile or pile-less surface, whose patterns are characteristic of Azerbaijan's many carpet-making regions. In November 2010 the Azerbaijani carpet was proclaimed a [Masterpiece of Intangible Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity) by UNESCO.[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336] Azerbaijani carpets can be categorized under several large groups and a multitude of subgroups. Scientific research of the Azerbaijani carpet is connected with the name of [Latif Karimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latif_Karimov), a prominent Soviet-era scientist and artist.[^cite_note-337]



Handwork coppery in [Lahij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lah%C4%B1c,_Ismailli)

Azerbaijan has been since ancient times known as a centre of a large variety of crafts. Archeology testifies to the well-developed agriculture, stock raising, metalworking, pottery, ceramics, and carpet-weaving that date as far back as to the 2nd millennium BC. Archeological sites in Dashbulaq, Hasansu, Zayamchai, and Tovuzchai uncovered from the BTC pipeline have revealed early Iron Age artifacts.[^cite_note-338]



A [miniature painting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miniature_\(illuminated_manuscript\)) of a battle scene on the walls of the [Palace of Shaki Khans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Shaki_Khans), 18th century, [city of Shaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaki,_Azerbaijan)

The [Gamigaya Petroglyphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamigaya_Petroglyphs), which date back to the 1st to 4th millennium BC, are located in Azerbaijan's [Ordubad District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordubad_District). They consist of some 1,500 dislodged and carved rock paintings with images of deer, goats, bulls, dogs, snakes, birds, fantastic beings, and people, carriages, and various symbols were found on basalt rocks.[^cite_note-339] Norwegian [ethnographer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnographer) and adventurer [Thor Heyerdahl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thor_Heyerdahl) was convinced that people from the area went to [Scandinavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scandinavia) in about 100 AD, took their boatbuilding skills with them, and transmuted them into the [Viking boats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longship) in northern Europe.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341]

Over the centuries, Azerbaijani art has gone through many stylistic changes. Painting is traditionally characterized by a warmth of colour and light, as exemplified in the works of [Azim Azimzade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azim_Azimzade) and [Bahruz Kangarli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahruz_Kangarli), and a preoccupation with religious figures and cultural motifs.[^cite_note-342] Azerbaijani painting enjoyed preeminence in Caucasus for hundreds of years, from the [Romanesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_art) and [Ottoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_Ottoman_Empire) periods, and through the [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_realism) and [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque) periods, the latter two of which saw fruition in Azerbaijan. Notable artists include [Sattar Bahlulzade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sattar_Bahlulzade), [Togrul Narimanbekov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Togrul_Narimanbekov), [Tahir Salahov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahir_Salahov), [Alakbar Rezaguliyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alakbar_Rezaguliyev), [Mirza Gadim Iravani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirza_Gadim_Iravani), [Mikayil Abdullayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikayil_Abdullayev) and [Boyukagha Mirzazade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boyukagha_Mirzazade).[^cite_note-343]

### Literature

The earliest known figure in written [Azerbaijani literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_literature) was [Izzeddin Hasanoghlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izzeddin_Hasanoghlu), who composed a [divan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diwan_\(poetry\)) consisting of Persian and Azerbaijani [ghazals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghazal).[^cite_note-beale-344][^cite_note-caferoglu-345] In Persian ghazals he used a pen-name, while his Azerbaijani ghazals were composed under his own name of Hasanoghlu.[^cite_note-beale-344] Among the medieval authors was Persian poet and philosopher [Nizami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizami_Ganjavi), called Ganjavi after his place of birth, [Ganja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganja,_Azerbaijan), who was the author of the [Khamsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamsa_of_Nizami) ("The Quintuplet"), composed of five romantic poems, including "The Treasure of Mysteries", "Khosrow and Shīrīn", and "Leyli and Mejnūn".[^cite_note-346]

Classical literature was formed in the 14th century based on the various Early Middle Ages dialects of [Tabriz](439791.md) and [Shirvan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirvan). Among the poets of this period were [Gazi Burhanaddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadi_Burhan_al-Din), Haqiqi (pen-name of [Jahan Shah Qara Qoyunlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jahan_Shah)), and [Habibi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habibi_\(poet\)).[^cite_note-tyrrell1-347] The end of the 14th century was the start of literary activity of [Imadaddin Nasimi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nesimi),[^cite_note-348] one of the greatest Azerbaijani[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-burrill-350][^cite_note-351] [Hurufi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurufism) mystical poets of the late 14th and early 15th centuries[^cite_note-nesimibritannica-352] and one of the most prominent early divan masters in Turkic literary history,[^cite_note-nesimibritannica-352] who also composed poetry in Persian[^cite_note-burrill-350][^cite_note-babinger-353] and Arabic.[^cite_note-nesimibritannica-352] The divan and ghazal styles were further developed by poets [Qasem-e Anvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qasem-e_Anvar), [Fuzuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuzuli_\(poet\)) and [Safavid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safavid_dynasty) [Shah Ismail I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Ismail_I) who wrote under the pen name "Khata'i".

The *[Book of Dede Korkut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Dede_Korkut)* consists of two manuscripts copied in the 16th century,[^cite_note-Meeker-354] and was not written earlier than the 15th century.[^cite_note-Kafadar-355][^cite_note-IranicaOguzKhan-356] It is a collection of 12 stories reflecting the oral tradition of Oghuz nomads.[^cite_note-IranicaOguzKhan-356] The 16th-century poet [Fuzuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuzuli_\(poet\)) produced his timeless philosophical and lyrical *Qazals* in Arabic, Persian, and Azerbaijani. Benefiting immensely from the fine literary traditions of his environment, and building upon the legacy of his predecessors, Fuzuli was destined to become the leading literary figure of his society. His major works include *The Divan of Ghazals* and *The Qasidas*. In the same century, Azerbaijani literature further flourished with the development of ashik ([Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language): *Aşıq*) poetic genre of bards. During the same period, under the pen-name of Khatāī ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): خطائی for *sinner*) Shah Ismail I wrote about 1,400 verses in Azerbaijani,[^cite_note-minorsky1-357] which were later published as his *Divan*. A unique literary style known as *qoshma* ([Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language): *qoşma* for *improvisation*) was introduced in this period and developed by Shah Ismail and later by his son and successor, Shah [Tahmasp I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahmasp_I).[^cite_note-Mino-358]

In the span of the 17th and 18th centuries, Fuzuli's unique genres as well ashik poetry were taken up by prominent poets and writers such as [Qovsi of Tabriz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qovsi_of_Tabriz), [Shah Abbas Sani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbas_II_of_Persia), [Agha Mesih Shirvani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agha_Mesih_Shirvani?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Molla Vali Vidadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molla_Vali_Vidadi), [Molla Panah Vagif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molla_Panah_Vagif), and others. Along with Turks, [Turkmens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkmens) and [Uzbeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzbeks), Azerbaijanis celebrate the [Epic of Koroglu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_of_Koroglu) (from [Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language): *kor oğlu* for *blind man's son*), a legendary folk hero.[^cite_note-samuel-359] Several documented versions of Koroglu epic remain at the Institute for Manuscripts of the [National Academy of Sciences of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Academy_of_Sciences_of_Azerbaijan).[^cite_note-caferoglu-345]

### Media and cinema

Main articles: [Media of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Azerbaijan), [Television in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Azerbaijan), and [Cinema of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Azerbaijan)

The first newspaper in Azerbaijani, *[Akinchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akinchi)*, was published in 1875.[^cite_note-360] There are three state-owned television channels: [AzTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AzTV), [Idman TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idman_Azerbaijan_TV) and [Medeniyyet TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medeniyyet_TV). There is one public channel and 6 private channels: [İctimai Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0ctimai_Television), Space TV, [Lider TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lider_TV), [Azad Azerbaijan TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azad_Azerbaijan_TV), [Xazar TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xazar_TV), [ Real TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_TV_\(Azerbaijan\)?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [ARB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Region_TV).[^cite_note-361]



Scene from the Azerbaijani film *[In the Kingdom of Oil and Millions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_the_Kingdom_of_Oil_and_Millions)*, 1916

The film industry in Azerbaijan dates back to 1898. Azerbaijan was among the first countries involved in cinematography,[^cite_note-mishon-362] with the apparatus first showing up in Baku.[^cite_note-cinemainazerbaijan-363] In 1919 a documentary *The Celebration of the Anniversary of Azerbaijani Independence* was filmed on the first anniversary of Azerbaijan's independence from Russia, 27 May, and premiered in June 1919 at several theatres in Baku.[^cite_note-ocaz-364] After the Soviet power was established in 1920, [Nariman Narimanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nariman_Narimanov), chairman of the Revolutionary Committee of Azerbaijan, signed a decree nationalizing Azerbaijan's cinema. This also influenced the creation of [Azerbaijani animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_animation).[^cite_note-ocaz-364]

In 1991, after Azerbaijan gained its independence from the Soviet Union, the first Baku International Film Festival East-West was held in Baku. In December 2000, former President Heydar Aliyev signed a decree proclaiming 2 August to be the professional holiday of filmmakers of Azerbaijan. Since the early 2020s, Azerbaijan-produced television series, such as *Əqrəb Mövsümü* (Scorpion Season), have experienced a notable rise in domestic and regional popularity, supported by improved production standards and broader genre diversity.[^cite_note-365]

[Media freedom in Azerbaijan](43480308.md) is repressed according to [Reporters without Borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reporters_without_Borders).[^cite_note-e402-366] Azerbaijan had the largest number of journalists imprisoned in Europe in 2015, according to the [Committee to Protect Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists), and is the 5th most censored country in the world, ahead of Iran and China.[^cite_note-367] Some critical journalists have been [arrested](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_misinformation#Efforts_to_combat_misinformation) for their coverage of the [COVID-19 pandemic in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Azerbaijan).[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] Journalists from the independent [Abzas Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abzas_Media), Toplum TV, and [Meydan TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meydan_TV) were prosecuted in 2024 and 2025 in trials that international human rights organizations described as unfair.[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372]

### Cuisine

[Azerbaijani cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_cuisine) uses an abundance of seasonal vegetables and greens. Fresh herbs, including mint, cilantro (coriander), dill, basil, parsley, tarragon, leeks, chives, thyme, marjoram, green onion, and watercress, are popular and often accompany main dishes on the table. Climatic diversity and fertility of the land are reflected in the national dishes, which are based on fish from the Caspian Sea, local meat (mainly mutton and beef), and seasonal vegetables and greens.

Saffron-rice [plov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plov) is the flagship food in Azerbaijan, and [black tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_tea) is the national beverage.[^cite_note-373] Azerbaijanis often use traditional [armudu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armudu) (pear-shaped) glass as they have very strong [tea culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_tea_culture).[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] Popular traditional dishes include [bozbash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bozbash) (lamb soup that exists in several regional varieties with the addition of different vegetables), [qutab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutab) (fried turnover with a filling of greens or minced meat) and [dushbara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dushbara) (dumplings filled with ground meat and spices).

### Sport

Main article: [Sport in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Azerbaijan)



[Shakhriyar Mamedyarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakhriyar_Mamedyarov) was the 2013 [World Rapid Chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Rapid_Chess_Championship) and three-time [European Team Chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Team_Chess_Championship) champion.



[Teimour Radjabov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teimour_Radjabov) was the 2019 [World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chess_World_Cup_2019) and three-time European Team Chess champion.

Freestyle wrestling has been traditionally regarded as Azerbaijan's [national sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_sport), in which Azerbaijan has won [fourteen medals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_at_the_Olympics), including four golds, since joining the [International Olympic Committee](15147.md). The most popular sports are [football](10568.md) and wrestling.[^cite_note-376]

The [Association of Football Federations of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Football_Federations_of_Azerbaijan), with 9,122 registered players, is the largest sporting association in the country.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] The [national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_national_football_team) demonstrates relatively low performance in the international arena compared to the nation's football clubs. The most successful clubs are [Neftçi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neft%C3%A7i_PFK), [Qarabağ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qaraba%C4%9F_FK), and [Gabala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabala_FK). In [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_UEFA_Europa_League), Neftchi Baku became the first Azerbaijani team to advance to the group stage of a European competition.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] In [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_UEFA_Europa_League), Qarabağ became the second Azerbaijani club advancing to the group stage of [UEFA Europa League](232175.md). In 2017, after beating [Copenhagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F.C._Copenhagen) 2–2 ([a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Away_goals_rule)) in the play-off round of the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_UEFA_Champions_League), Qarabağ became the first Azerbaijani club to reach the group stage.[^cite_note-381]

[Futsal](188197.md) is another popular sport in Azerbaijan. The [Azerbaijan national futsal team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_national_futsal_team) reached fourth place in the [2010 UEFA Futsal Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_UEFA_Futsal_Championship), while domestic club [Araz Naxçivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Araz_Nax%C3%A7ivan) clinched bronze medals at the [2009–10 UEFA Futsal Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_UEFA_Futsal_Cup) and [2013–14 UEFA Futsal Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_UEFA_Futsal_Cup).[^cite_note-382] Azerbaijan was the main sponsor of Spanish football club [Atlético de Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_de_Madrid) during seasons 2013/2014 and 2014/2015, a partnership that the club described should 'promote the image of Azerbaijan in the world'.[^cite_note-383]

Azerbaijan has hosted many international [chess](5134.md) tournaments and competitions and became [European Team Chess Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Team_Chess_Championship) winners in 2009, 2013 and 2017.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386] Notable chess players include [Teimour Radjabov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teimour_Radjabov), [Shahriyar Mammadyarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahriyar_Mammadyarov), [Vladimir Makogonov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Makogonov), [Vugar Gashimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vugar_Gashimov) and former [World Chess Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Chess_Champion) [Garry Kasparov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garry_Kasparov). As of 2014, country's home of [Shamkir Chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamkir_Chess) a category 22 event and one of the highest rated tournaments of all time.[^cite_note-387] [Backgammon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backgammon) also plays a major role in Azerbaijani culture.[^cite_note-388] The game is very popular in Azerbaijan and is widely played among the local public.[^cite_note-389] There are also different variations of backgammon developed and analysed by Azerbaijani experts.[^cite_note-390]



[Baku National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_National_Stadium) was used for the first [European Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Games) in June 2015.

[Azerbaijan women's national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_women's_national_football_team) secured a fourth-place finish at the [2005 European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Women's_European_Volleyball_Championship).[^cite_note-391] Over the last years, clubs like [Rabita Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabita_Baku) and [Azerrail Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerrail_Baku) achieved great success at European cups.[^cite_note-392] Azerbaijani volleyball players include likes of [Valeriya Korotenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valeriya_Korotenko), [Oksana Parkhomenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oksana_Parkhomenko), [Inessa Korkmaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inessa_Korkmaz), [Natalya Mammadova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalya_Mammadova), and [Alla Hasanova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alla_Hasanova).

Other Azerbaijani athletes are [Namig Abdullayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namig_Abdullayev), [Toghrul Asgarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toghrul_Asgarov), [Rovshan Bayramov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rovshan_Bayramov), [Sharif Sharifov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharif_Sharifov), [Mariya Stadnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariya_Stadnik) and [Farid Mansurov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farid_Mansurov) in [wrestling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amateur_wrestling), [Nazim Huseynov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazim_Huseynov), [Elnur Mammadli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elnur_Mammadli), [Elkhan Mammadov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkhan_Mammadov_\(judoka\)) and [Rustam Orujov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rustam_Orujov) in judo, [Rafael Aghayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Aghayev) in karate, [Magomedrasul Majidov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magomedrasul_Majidov) and [Aghasi Mammadov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aghasi_Mammadov) in [boxing](4243.md), [Nizami Pashayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizami_Pashayev) in [Olympic weightlifting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_weightlifting), [Azad Asgarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azad_Asgarov) in [pankration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pankration), [Eduard Mammadov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard_Mammadov) in kickboxing, and [K-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K-1) fighter [Zabit Samedov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zabit_Samedov).

Azerbaijan has a [Formula One racetrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_City_Circuit), constructed in 2012,[^cite_note-393] and the country hosted its first [Formula One Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_European_Grand_Prix) in 2016[^cite_note-394] and the [Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix) since 2017. Other annual sporting events held in the country are the [Baku Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Cup) tennis tournament and the [Tour d'Azerbaïdjan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_d'Azerba%C3%AFdjan) cycling race.

Azerbaijan hosted several major sport competitions since the late 2000s, including the [2013 F1 Powerboat World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_F1_Powerboat_World_Championship_season), [2012 FIFA U-17 Women's World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_FIFA_U-17_Women's_World_Cup), [2011 AIBA World Boxing Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_AIBA_World_Boxing_Championships), [2010 European Wrestling Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_European_Wrestling_Championships), [2009 Rhythmic Gymnastics European Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Rhythmic_Gymnastics_European_Championships), [2014 European Taekwondo Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Taekwondo_Championships), [2014 Rhythmic Gymnastics European Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Rhythmic_Gymnastics_European_Championships), and [2016 World Chess Olympiad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/42nd_Chess_Olympiad).[^cite_note-395] Baku was selected to host the [2015 European Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_European_Games).[^cite_note-396] Baku hosted the fourth [Islamic Solidarity Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Solidarity_Games) in 2017[^cite_note-397] and the [2019 European Youth Summer Olympic Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_European_Youth_Summer_Olympic_Festival),[^cite_note-398] and was a host of [UEFA Euro 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2020).[^cite_note-399]

[^cite_note-LaPorte2016-5]: &#x20;LaPorte, Jody (2016). "Semi-presidentialism in Azerbaijan". In [Elgie, Robert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Elgie_\(academic\)); [Moestrup, Sophia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophia_Moestrup?action=edit\&redlink=1) (eds.). *Semi-Presidentialism in the Caucasus and Central Asia*. London: Palgrave Macmillan (published 15 May 2016). pp. 91–117. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1057/978-1-137-38781-3_4](https://doi.org/10.1057%2F978-1-137-38781-3_4). [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-1-137-38780-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-1-137-38780-6). [LCCN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LCCN_\(identifier\)) [2016939393](https://lccn.loc.gov/2016939393). [OCLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OCLC_\(identifier\)) [6039791976](https://search.worldcat.org/oclc/6039791976). LaPorte examines the dynamics of semi-presidentialism in Azerbaijan. Azerbaijan's regime is a curious hybrid, in which semi-presidential institutions operate in the larger context of authoritarianism. The author compares formal Constitutional provisions with the practice of politics in the country, suggesting that formal and informal sources of authority come together to enhance the effective powers of the presidency. In addition to the considerable formal powers laid out in the Constitution, Azerbaijan's president also benefits from the support of the ruling party and informal family and patronage networks. LaPorte concludes by discussing the theoretical implications of this symbiosis between formal and informal institutions in Azerbaijan's semi-presidential regime.

[^cite_note-Meeker-354]: &#x20;Michael E. Meeker, "The Dede Korkut Ethic", International Journal of Middle East Studies, Vol. 24, No. 3 (Aug. 1992), 395–417. excerpt: The Book of Dede Korkut is an early record of oral Turkic folktales in Anatolia, and as such, one of the mythic charters of Turkish nationalist ideology. The oldest versions of the Book of Dede Korkut consist of two manuscripts copied in the 16th century. The twelve stories that are recorded in these manuscripts are believed to be derived from a cycle of stories and songs circulating among Turkic peoples living in northeastern Anatolia and northwestern Azerbaijan. According to Lewis (1974), an older substratum of these oral traditions dates to conflicts between the ancient Oghuz and their Turkish rivals in Central Asia (the Pecheneks and the Kipchaks), but this substratum has been clothed in references to the 14th-century campaigns of the Akkoyunlu Confederation of Turkic tribes against the Georgians, the Abkhaz, and the Greeks in Trebizond. Such stories and songs would have emerged no earlier than the beginning of the 13th century, and the written versions that have reached us would have been composed no later than the beginning of the 15th century. By this time, the Turkic peoples in question had been in touch with Islamic civilization for several centuries, had come to call themselves "Turcoman" rather than "Oghuz," had close associations with sedentary and urbanized societies, and were participating in Islamized regimes that included nomads, farmers, and townsmen. Some had abandoned their nomadic way of life altogether.

[^cite_note-Kafadar-355]: &#x20;Cemal Kafadar(1995), "in Between Two Worlds: Construction of the Ottoman states", University of California Press, 1995. Excerpt: "It was not earlier than the fifteenth century. Based on the fact that the author is buttering up both the Akkoyunlu and Ottoman rulers, it has been suggested that the composition belongs to someone living in the undefined border region lands between the two states during the reign of Uzun Hassan (1466–78). G. Lewis, on the other hand, dates the composition "fairly early in the 15th century at least."
